# Apple Silicon Port Factory (Experimental)

[![CI](https://github.com/mweinbach/apple-silicon-port-factory/actions/workflows/ci.yml/badge.svg?branch=main)](https://github.com/mweinbach/apple-silicon-port-factory/actions/workflows/ci.yml)

**[Project board](https://mweinbach.craft.me/aspc-whats-next)** — what is being worked on, known
issues, and proposed optimizations, each tied to the code it would touch.

> [!WARNING]
> **Experimental Research Project — Bring Your Own Legally Owned Games Only**
>
> This repository is an **experimental** open-source runtime, binary translator, and porting
> research framework aimed at running **legitimate Windows x86-64 games that you already legally
> own** natively on Apple Silicon (`aarch64-apple-darwin` macOS and `aarch64-apple-ios` iOS/iPadOS)
> using Metal 4 and MetalFX — **with no Wine, CrossOver, or D3DMetal at runtime**.
>
> - **No game files are distributed here.** No copyrighted executables, DLLs, shaders, audio banks,
>   textures, or game assets are included. You must supply your own legally purchased Windows game
>   installs.
> - **Experimental status.** Expect rough edges, missing Win32/COM/graphics APIs, and per-game
>   compatibility gaps. Pull requests adding new game targets, Win32 APIs, x86-64 instructions, or
>   graphics/audio fixes are welcome (see [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md)).
> - **No anti-cheat or DRM circumvention.** Kernel anti-cheat requirements are detected and
>   reported honestly (`cargo opsdb -- anticheat-audit`); bypasses are explicitly out of scope.
> - **No affiliation.** This project is independent and is not affiliated with, endorsed by, or
>   supported by Apple, Microsoft, Remedy Entertainment, Blizzard Entertainment, Valve, NVIDIA, AMD,
>   Audiokinetic, or RAD Game Tools. All trademarks belong to their respective owners.

---

## Architecture

This is a single, self-contained monorepo (zero git submodules; `db/` catalogs are plain text and
only the large Alan Wake 2 analysis tables are stored via Git LFS). Universal runtime code lives in `crates/`; per-game manifests and
opt-in game targets live in `games/<id>/` (currently including **Overwatch** and **Alan Wake 2**,
plus a starter template at [`games/_template/game.toml`](games/_template/game.toml)).

- **Universal ARM64 execution (`crates/x86-runtime`, `crates/port-loader`, `crates/pe-image`)** —
  Loads Windows PE32+ executables and DLLs, translates x86-64 to ARM64/NEON through an `iced-x86`
  interpreter, block JIT, relocatable `.aot` disk cache (`--prewarm-aot`), and static iOS Mach-O
  `.aot.o` emitter, with MSVC FH4/SEH unwinding. The default `port-runner` builds independently of game-specific crates.
  [Current instruction and export counts](docs/STATUS.md#catalog-totals)
- **Native Win32 & OS layer (`crates/win32-rt`)** — Clean-room Windows and middleware DLL exports
  built directly on macOS/iOS primitives: Mach/POSIX threads & sync, `~/Translator` virtual `C:\`
  filesystem, AppKit/UIKit surfaces, raw input, `GameController` XInput + CoreHaptics rumble,
  `IOHIDManager` DualSense/SetupDi, Winsock2, `rustls`-backed Schannel TLS 1.2/1.3, and WASAPI on
  CoreAudio.
- **Multi-API Metal 4 graphics (`crates/d3d12-metal`, `crates/dxil-metal`, `crates/win32-rt`)** —
  - **Direct3D 12**: Metal 4 backend with DXR 1.1 ray tracing, mesh shaders, geometry shader
    emulation, GPU indirect command buffers, and native `MTL4FXSpatialScaler` /
    `MTL4FXTemporalScaler` MetalFX upscaling.
  - **Direct3D 11.0 / 11.1**: COM device, immediate/deferred contexts, and SM 4/5 shaders routed
    through `libdxilconv` → `dxil-metal` → Metal 4.
  - **Vulkan**: Dynamic `libMoltenVK.dylib` bridge translating `VK_KHR_win32_surface` to
    `VK_EXT_metal_surface` (`CAMetalLayer`) + `VK_KHR_portability_enumeration`.
  - **Shaders**: DXIL SM 6.x (Apple Metal Shader Converter 4.0.1), FXC SM 4/5 (`libdxilconv`), and
    SPIR-V/WGSL (`naga`), with portable `.metallib` and `MTL4Archive` PSO caches.
- **Audio (`crates/win32-rt/src/audio`, `crates/wwise-audio`)** — CoreAudio-backed WASAPI shim
  with device hotplug and real-time Mach scheduling for any game, plus an optional standalone Rust
  Wwise 2022.1 (v145) `.bnk`/`.wem` runtime.
- **iOS / iPadOS host (`ios/PortHost`)** — UIKit + `CAMetalLayer` reference host with touch
  gamepad overlay, hardware/software keyboard bridge, Taptic Engine rumble passthrough, and
  zero-`MAP_JIT` static `.aot.o` execution.
- **Factory CLI & Diagnostic Toolkit (`crates/port-factory`)** — PE scanner, x86-64 → ARM64 ops
  catalog, Win32 import auditor, anti-cheat / .NET / shader / runtime-hazard auditors, offline
  `.aot` prewarming, scaffolding tools (`scaffold-op`, `scaffold-dll`), macOS `.app` and iOS
  `.app`/`.ipa` bundlers, and 4-tier x86 differential verification.
  Run `cargo opsdb -- readiness /path/to/game` to rank static CPU and import blockers before booting.
  [Readiness report](docs/OPS_DB.md#readiness-for-one-game)
- **Per-game targets (`games/`)** — Declarative `game.toml` manifests
  ([`games/_template/game.toml`](games/_template/game.toml),
  [`games/overwatch/game.toml`](games/overwatch/game.toml),
  [`games/alan-wake-2/game.toml`](games/alan-wake-2/game.toml)), plus Alan Wake 2's opt-in
  bespoke decompilation/lifting target under `games/alan-wake-2/`.

---

## Prerequisites & Quick Start

1. **Clone the repository.** Git LFS is optional: the universal runner, the test suite and
   `cargo opsdb -- doctor` work from a plain clone. Only the large Alan Wake 2 analysis tables
   (`db/progress.tsv`, `db/throws.tsv`, `db/dll_progress.tsv`, `db/ret_liveness*.tsv`) live in
   Git LFS.
   ```sh
   git clone https://github.com/mweinbach/apple-silicon-port-factory.git
   cd apple-silicon-port-factory

   # Only for Alan Wake 2 decompilation work:
   brew install git-lfs && git lfs install && git lfs pull
   ```
2. **Install Apple Metal Shader Converter 4.0.1** (downloaded from Apple Developer) and verify
   host prerequisites:
   ```sh
   ./scripts/install_metal_shader_converter.sh /path/to/Metal_Shader_Converter.pkg
   ./scripts/setup.sh
   ```
   *(Optional)* Fetch upstream reference repositories (`directx-headers`, `vgmstream`, etc.) into
   the gitignored `third_party/` folder with plain shallow clones (no git submodules):
   ```sh
   ./scripts/third_party.sh            # minimal (~35 MB: directx-headers + vgmstream)
   ./scripts/third_party.sh --all      # all optional reference repos (~2.7 GB)
   ```
3. **Build the universal ARM64 runner (~17 MB, ~15s):**
   ```sh
   cargo runner-build
   ```
4. **Audit, prewarm, and launch a Windows game you already own:**
   ```sh
   # Audit Win32 imports, x86-64 instruction coverage, and anti-cheat compatibility
   cargo opsdb -- win32-audit /path/to/YourGame
   cargo opsdb -- scan /path/to/YourGame
   cargo opsdb -- anticheat-audit /path/to/YourGame/Game.exe

   # Prewarm ARM64 .aot block cache and run with a game.toml manifest
   cargo runner -- --manifest games/overwatch/game.toml --game-dir /path/to/Overwatch --prewarm-aot
   cargo runner -- --manifest games/overwatch/game.toml --game-dir /path/to/Overwatch

   # Or run directly against any Windows x86-64 .exe / .dll without a manifest
   cargo runner -- --pe /path/to/app.exe --ops-report --no-run
   cargo runner -- --pe /path/to/library.dll --call-export ExportName
   ```
5. **Run the universal factory test suite:**
   ```sh
   cargo factory-test -- --test-threads=1
   cargo opsdb -- doctor
   ```
6. **Alan Wake 2 opt-in target (`games/alan-wake-2/`):**
   ```sh
   export AW2_GAME_DIR=/path/to/your/AlanWake2
   cargo loader -- --game-dir "$AW2_GAME_DIR"
   cargo data status
   ./tools/fndb.sh report
   ```

---

## Adding a New Game & Contributing

We want this framework to be easy to expand — adding support for a new game usually requires
**zero game-specific Rust code**:

1. Copy [`games/_template/game.toml`](games/_template/game.toml) to `games/<game-id>/game.toml`
   and fill in `[app]` (`id`, `name`, `exe`, `win_dir`, optional `steam_app_id`) and `[runtime]`.
2. Audit your install (`cargo opsdb -- win32-audit`, `scan`, `anticheat-audit`, `runtime-audit`).
3. Prewarm and boot with `cargo runner -- --manifest games/<game-id>/game.toml --game-dir /path/to/Game`.
4. Fix any real universal blockers you hit in `crates/win32-rt`, `crates/x86-runtime`,
   `crates/d3d12-metal`, or `crates/dxil-metal` so every future game benefits too.
5. Open a PR! See [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md) and [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) for architecture
   rules, Win32 API sync steps (`cargo opsdb -- win32-sync`), and the PR checklist.

---

# AGENTS.md

This repo (**Apple Silicon Port Factory**) is an **experimental** native Apple Silicon
(`aarch64-apple-darwin` / `aarch64-apple-ios`) porting runtime and research toolkit for running
**legitimate Windows x86-64 games that you already legally own**. It loads user-supplied Windows
`.exe` and `.dll` files, translates x86-64 machine code into ARM64, handles Win32/CRT/COM calls in
Rust, converts shaders to Metal, and renders on Metal 4 with **no Wine, CrossOver, or D3DMetal at
runtime**.

It started as an Alan Wake 2 port and grew into a general-purpose, single-repo runtime (zero git
submodules; `db/` catalogs are plain text, with only the large Alan Wake 2 analysis tables in Git
LFS). The universal engine lives under `crates/`; everything
specific to a single game lives under `games/<id>/` (including `games/overwatch/`,
`games/alan-wake-2/`, and starter template `games/_template/game.toml`). Nothing in this repo
distributes game binaries or assets.

---

## How a Windows game runs here

When you run `port-runner` on a Windows `.exe`, five layers do the work:

1. **PE loading (`crates/pe-image`, `crates/port-loader`)** — Maps the `.exe` and its non-builtin
   DLLs into memory, applies relocations, sets up static TLS, and synthesizes minimal fake PE
   headers/export tables for our builtin DLLs (some middleware walks PE export directories by hand
   instead of calling `GetProcAddress`).
2. **CPU execution (`crates/x86-runtime`)** — Translates guest x86-64 code into ARM64/NEON blocks
   via an `iced-x86` decoder, block JIT, and persistent `.aot` cache on disk (`--prewarm-aot`
   compiles blocks ahead of time). Handles Win64 ↔ AAPCS64 calls, `VA_DISPATCH`, and MSVC
   FH4/SEH exceptions.
3. **Win32 & OS layer (`crates/win32-rt`)** — Implements Windows and middleware DLL exports ([catalog totals](docs/STATUS.md#catalog-totals))
   directly on macOS primitives: Mach/POSIX threads and waits, AppKit windows and HID/
   GameController input, CoreAudio for WASAPI, a `~/Translator` virtual `C:\` filesystem, plus
   Direct3D 12, Direct3D 11, and Vulkan entry points.
4. **Shaders (`crates/dxil-metal`)** — Converts DXIL SM 6.x through Apple's Metal Shader Converter
   (`libmetalirconverter.dylib`), legacy FXC SM 4/5 through `libdxilconv.dylib`, and SPIR-V/WGSL
   through `naga`. Caches both `.metallib` output and compiled `MTL4Archive` pipelines.
5. **GPU execution (`crates/d3d12-metal`)** — Drives Metal 4 directly: command queues/lists,
   residency sets, descriptor tables, mesh shaders, DXR 1.1 ray tracing, GPU indirect command
   buffers, and `MTL4FXSpatialScaler` MetalFX upscaling.

Per-game overrides sit on top of this stack. Most games only need a declarative `games/<id>/game.toml`
manifest (see `games/overwatch/game.toml`). Alan Wake 2 also has an optional bespoke decompilation
target (`games/alan-wake-2/`, enabled via `port-loader --features aw2 --bin aw2-loader`).

---

## Hard rules (and why they exist)

Read these before changing code. Every rule here comes from something that actually broke.

1. **Keep `crates/` completely game-agnostic.**
   Never put game-specific virtual addresses, hardcoded game names, autoplay bots, or engine-specific
   asset parsers in `crates/`. Configure per-game behavior through `TargetManifest` (`game.toml`),
   `win32_rt::config::AppConfig`, `DllRegistry`/`ComRegistry`, `x86_runtime::hooks`, or middleware
   hook traits (`CohtmlViewHook`, `CompletionTaskHook`, `PresentHook`). All Alan Wake 2 code lives
   under `games/alan-wake-2/`.

2. **Never import `objc2-metal` in `win32-rt`.**
   All Metal 4 calls belong inside `d3d12-metal` (and `dxil-metal` for PSO archives). Even D3D11
   runs through `d3d12-metal`'s safe Rust types, and Vulkan loads `libMoltenVK.dylib` dynamically.
   Keeping Metal behind `d3d12-metal` stops COM shims from turning into a tangle of raw Objective-C
   calls.

3. **Be honest about stubs, errors, and test results.**
   Never fake success, silently `memcpy` past an unimplemented API, fabricate telemetry, or inflate
   coverage numbers. If an API isn't implemented yet, log it through `stub!` / `failing!` and return
   the documented Windows failure code. Never claim a fix works without running the relevant build
   or test and reporting what actually happened.

4. **Never write mutable data to executable pages.**
   ARM64 enforces W^X and kills the process; Rosetta treats writes to code pages as self-modifying
   code and stalls in `com.apple.rosetta.exceptionserver` (that once cost 40 seconds on every boot).
   Keep JIT/AOT code pages strictly separate from RW data, counters, and vtables.

5. **Assume all guest memory is unaligned.**
   x86-64 doesn't care if a `u64` sits at a 4-byte offset or a `u16` sits at an odd address. Rust
   does, and debug builds abort on misaligned typed derefs. Always read and write guest memory via
   `exe::rd::<T>`, `exe::wr::<T>`, `ptr::read_unaligned` / `write_unaligned`, or `#[repr(C, packed)]`.

6. **Never stub out shipping decompressors or swap game shaders.**
   Let real compression DLLs (`oo2core_*`, `zstd`, `lz4` when shipped) run through the ARM64 JIT
   unless you have a real, tested native implementation. Never substitute game shaders, and keep
   engine-specific GPU barrier shortcuts (like `AW2_IB_BINDLESS_WAR`) **OFF by default**.

7. **Every line of decompiled game code must trace to the binary.**
   In `games/alan-wake-2/crates/aw2-game`, never guess behavior or copy from old prototypes without
   re-verifying. Check the original disassembly/Ghidra output and prove parity with a differential
   test against the x86-64 oracle.

8. **Never commit game files, `.app` bundles, or personal machine paths.**
   No `.exe`, `.dll`, `.rmdblob`, `.rmdtoc`, `.binrfx`, `.bnk`, `.wem`, `.bk2`, `*.app/`, Steam HTML
   dumps, or `/Users/<name>` paths anywhere in code or docs.

---

## Where code belongs

Use this lookup table before you create or edit a file:

| Problem or task | Where it goes |
|---|---|
| Missing or broken Win32/CRT/COM/middleware export (`UNIMPLEMENTED foo.dll!Bar`) | `crates/win32-rt/src/` (then run `cargo opsdb -- win32-sync`) |
| Windowing, AppKit, mouse/keyboard/raw input, XInput, DualSense HID | `crates/win32-rt/src/user32/`, `macos/`, `misc/{xinput1_4,hid,setupapi}.rs` |
| WASAPI / CoreAudio / `mmdevapi` / `avrt` | `crates/win32-rt/src/audio/`, `misc/{mmdevapi,avrt}.rs` |
| Direct3D 12 COM shim vs Metal 4 implementation | COM vtables in `crates/win32-rt/src/d3d12/`; Metal 4 + MetalFX in `crates/d3d12-metal/` |
| Direct3D 11.0/11.1 runtime | `crates/win32-rt/src/d3d11/` (backed by `d3d12-metal` + `dxil-metal`) |
| Vulkan loader & `VK_KHR_win32_surface` bridge | `crates/win32-rt/src/misc/vulkan_1.rs` (loads `libMoltenVK.dylib`) |
| Shader translation (DXIL, FXC SM 4/5, SPIR-V, WGSL) or PSO cache | `crates/dxil-metal/` |
| x86-64 decoder, ARM64/NEON block JIT, `.aot` cache, FH4/SEH unwinding | `crates/x86-runtime/` |
| PE32+ / .NET metadata parsing | `crates/pe-image/` |
| Win64 ↔ AAPCS64 proc-macro ABI shims | `crates/win64-abi/` |
| Universal Wwise 2022.1 `.bnk`/`.wem` parser, Vorbis decoder, DSP engine | `crates/wwise-audio/` |
| Universal runner CLI (`port-runner`), `game.toml` loading, PE mapping | `crates/port-loader/` |
| Ops DB, Win32 DB, PE scanner, `aot-prewarm`, Steam `.app` bundler | `crates/port-factory/` (`cargo opsdb`) |
| Offline D3D12 trace inspector & Metal 4 replay | `crates/d3d12-replay/` |
| Adding a new game target | `games/<id>/game.toml` (start declarative; don't add Rust crates unless truly needed) |
| Alan Wake 2 decomp, lifted shards, Northlight VFS, AW2 hooks/perf logs | `games/alan-wake-2/` |

---

## Fast build, run, and test commands

The default universal runner (`port-runner`) builds without compiling the Alan Wake 2 shards. Stay on `port-runner` and `cargo factory-test` unless
you are explicitly working on Alan Wake 2.

```sh
# Build & run the universal runner
cargo runner-build
cargo runner -- --manifest games/overwatch/game.toml --game-dir /path/to/Overwatch
cargo runner -- --pe /path/to/any.exe --prewarm-aot          # offline ARM64 .aot block precompile
cargo runner -- --pe /path/to/any.exe --ops-report --no-run  # audit instructions + unresolved imports

# Fast test suites (always pass --test-threads=1 for win32-rt / factory-test)
cargo factory-test -- --test-threads=1
cargo test -p d3d12-metal --lib --target aarch64-apple-darwin -- --test-threads=1
cargo test -p win32-rt --lib --target aarch64-apple-darwin -- d3d11 misc::vulkan_1 --test-threads=1

# Factory CLI (crates/port-factory) & Diagnostic Toolkit
cargo opsdb -- stats
cargo opsdb -- win32-sync
cargo opsdb -- win32-audit /path/to/game/
cargo opsdb -- runtime-audit /path/to/game/        # static MXCSR/x87/AVX-narrow/sync/RWX hazard scan
cargo opsdb -- verify-x86 /path/to/game/ --oracle  # offline 4-tier diff verify + Rosetta 2 oracle
cargo opsdb -- aot-prewarm /path/to/game.exe
cargo opsdb -- scan /path/to/game/ --record

# Alan Wake 2 full target (links aw2-game shards + Wwise native bridge)
cargo loader-build
cargo loader -- --game-dir "$AW2_GAME_DIR"
cargo check -p aw2-game --no-default-features --target aarch64-apple-darwin  # fast check of ns/ (~12s)
cargo data status
tools/fndb.sh report

# Legacy Rosetta 2 x86_64 build (kept for differential testing)
cargo rosetta-build
cargo hybrid-test
```

Key host paths and env vars:
- **Metal Shader Converter 4.0.1**: `~/.local/opt/metal-shaderconverter` (override with `METAL_IRCONVERTER_DYLIB`). Out-of-process worker: `cargo build --release -p dxil-metal --bin dxil-metald`.
- **Virtual Windows root (`C:\`)**: `~/Translator` (`TRANSLATOR_HOME` or `AW2_DRIVE_C`).
- **AOT block cache**: `~/Library/Caches/port-factory/aot` (`PORT_AOT_DIR`, fallback `AW2_DLL_AOT_DIR`).
- **Primary env prefix**: use `PORT_*` for new knobs (`PORT_METALFX=0`, `PORT_D3D11=0`, `PORT_AOT_DIR`, `PORT_HEADLESS=1`, `PORT_MANIFEST`, `PORT_GAME_DIR`). Legacy `AW2_*` aliases still work where kept for compatibility.

---

## Repo gotchas (save yourself an hour)

- **`win32-rt/build.rs` stops at the first `#[cfg(test)]`.**
  The export scanner splits every source file on the first `#[cfg(test)]` token. If you put a
  `#[cfg(test)]` helper above an `extern "win64"` export, every export below it silently disappears
  from the DLL table. Keep `#[cfg(test)]` modules at the bottom of the file.
- **Always run `win32-rt` audio tests with `-- --test-threads=1`.**
  CoreAudio / `akrec` tests share process-global audio state and deadlock under Rust's default
  parallel test runner.
- **Sync `db/win32_apis.tsv` whenever you add or move `win32-rt` exports.**
  Run `cargo opsdb -- win32-sync` and commit the updated TSV alongside your Rust change.
- **Avoid building `aw2-loader` unless you touched AW2.**
  `aw2-game` links hundreds of generated shard files. For universal changes, build `port-runner`
  (`cargo runner-build`) and run `cargo check -p aw2-game --no-default-features` if you changed a
  shared trait or signature in `x86-runtime` or `win32-rt`.
- **Zsh `$path` trap.**
  The shell is zsh, where lowercase `path` is tied to `$PATH`. Never write `path=...` in shell
  commands.
- **Never `process launch` Rosetta binaries under `lldb`.**
  Launching an x86-64 binary directly under lldb can wedge Apple's `oahd` daemon machine-wide. Use
  `tools/lldb.sh`, which attaches by PID after the image is mapped.
- **Read the actual code before trusting old comments or memos.**
  `docs/design/` contains historical design notes from earlier phases.
  Treat current source files and tests as the source of truth.

---

## Standard workflows & repo skills

Detailed step-by-step runbooks live in `.agents/skills/`:

| Skill | When to use it |
|---|---|
| `bring-up-game` | Auditing a new Windows game, writing `games/<id>/game.toml`, prewarming `.aot`, and triaging boot blockers |
| `implement-win32-api` | Implementing or fixing Win32, CRT, COM, or middleware exports in `crates/win32-rt` |
| `implement-x86-op` | Adding, promoting, or fixing x86-64 → ARM64/NEON instructions in `crates/x86-runtime` and `ops_db` |
| `debug-runtime-and-nan` | Hunting CPU/NaN/MXCSR/AOT/crash bugs with `runtime-audit`, `verify-x86`, `live-probe`, `bisect-runtime`, `nan-trace`, Rosetta `oracle`, and `tools/lldb.sh` |
| `graphics-metal4` | Working on D3D12, D3D11, Vulkan→MoltenVK, Metal 4, MetalFX, or `d3d12-replay` |
| `shader-pipeline` | Working on `crates/dxil-metal`, `dxil-metald`, `libdxilconv`, `naga`, Tier-1/Tier-2 PSO caches, and `PsoManifest` |
| `package-macos-and-ios` | Building & verifying portable macOS `.app` bundles, iOS `.app`/`.ipa` (static `.aot.o`), Steam ports, `optimize-assets`, and `ios/PortHost` |
| `audio-and-windowing` | Working on AppKit/UIKit windowing & deadlock rules, raw input, gamepads/DualSense HID, WASAPI→CoreAudio, and `wwise-audio` |
| `profile-and-verify` | Profiling boot/CPU/hitches/GPU passes and running pre-PR boundary, `cargo opsdb -- doctor`, and test gates |
| `audit-repo-hygiene` | Auditing copyright/privacy hygiene, crate boundaries, `db/*.tsv` storage (plain-text catalogs, Git LFS for large tables), and zero-submodule cleanliness before PRs or pushes |
| `decompile-function` | Bespoke Alan Wake 2 function decompilation under `games/alan-wake-2/crates/aw2-game` |

### Bringing up a new Windows game

See `.agents/skills/bring-up-game/SKILL.md`. In short:
1. Audit the install first:
   ```sh
   cargo opsdb -- scan /path/to/game/
   cargo opsdb -- win32-audit /path/to/game/
   cargo opsdb -- anticheat-audit /path/to/game/Game.exe
   cargo opsdb -- app-audit /path/to/game/Game.exe
   cargo opsdb -- runtime-audit /path/to/game/
   ```
2. Create `games/<id>/game.toml` (copy `games/_template/game.toml` as a template). Set `name`,
   `exe`, `win_dir`, optional `steam_app_id`, and runtime flags.
3. Prewarm the ARM64 `.aot` cache and boot with `port-runner`:
   ```sh
   cargo runner -- --manifest games/<id>/game.toml --game-dir /path/to/game --prewarm-aot
   cargo runner -- --manifest games/<id>/game.toml --game-dir /path/to/game
   ```
4. Fix the real blockers you hit, one at a time. Missing Win32 APIs go in `crates/win32-rt` with a
   unit test + `cargo opsdb -- win32-sync`. Missing x86 instructions go in `crates/x86-runtime`. Do
   not hardcode game workarounds in universal crates.

### Adding or fixing a Win32 API

See `.agents/skills/implement-win32-api/SKILL.md` for the step-by-step checklist. In short:
reproduce the missing/failing call, verify the exact Windows ABI and types, implement real macOS
behavior (or an honest `stub!` failure), add a unit test, run `cargo opsdb -- win32-sync`, and
verify with `cargo test -p win32-rt --lib --target aarch64-apple-darwin -- <module> --test-threads=1`.

### Decompiling an Alan Wake 2 function

See `.agents/skills/decompile-function/SKILL.md`. In short: inspect via `tools/fndb.sh show` and
`tools/ghidra_decompile.sh`, implement under `games/alan-wake-2/crates/aw2-game/src/ns/` using
`exe::rd`/`exe::wr`, register with accurate `Throws::{No, Discard, Caught}`, prove parity with
`--diff-test`, run `tools/fndb.sh sync`, and commit code + `db/progress.tsv` + `db/throws.tsv`.

---

## Definition of done

Before you finish a change or commit:

1. **Right crate, clean boundary** — Universal code stays in `crates/`; `win32-rt` has zero
   `objc2-metal` and zero `aw2-data`; `port-runner` has zero `aw2-game` or `aw2-data`.
2. **Real verification** — Run the narrowest tests that cover your change, plus `cargo factory-test
   -- --test-threads=1` and `cargo runner-build` for cross-cutting edits. If you touched `win32-rt`
   exports, run `cargo opsdb -- win32-sync`. CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) repeats the hygiene
   audit, `doctor`, `runner-build`, `factory-test`, the AOT verify test, the `aw2-game` check and
   an iOS library check on every pull request, but it has no physical GPU: Metal 4, audio and
   end-to-end tests still have to be run locally.
3. **Zero warnings in code you touched** — Fix unused helpers, dead code, and compiler warnings
   before committing.
4. **Small, clear commits** — Format: `<area>: <imperative summary>` (`factory`, `x86-runtime`,
   `win32`, `loader`, `d3d12`, `d3d11`, `vulkan`, `shaders`, `audio`, `pe`, `game/<ns>`, `tools`,
   `docs`, `build`). Keep `main` green on every commit.

---

[workspace]
resolver = "2"
members = [
    "crates/win64-abi",
    "crates/bink2-codec",
    "games/alan-wake-2/crates/aw2-data",
    "crates/dxil-metal",
    "crates/pe-image",
    "crates/port-manifest",
    "crates/port-factory",
    "crates/win32-rt",
    "crates/x86-runtime",
    "games/alan-wake-2/crates/aw2-game",
    "crates/port-loader",
    "crates/wwise-audio",
    "crates/d3d12-metal",
    "crates/d3d12-replay",
]
exclude = ["third_party"]

[workspace.package]
edition = "2021"
version = "0.1.0"
license = "MIT OR Apache-2.0"
repository = "https://github.com/mweinbach/apple-silicon-port-factory"
description = "Experimental clean-room Windows x86-64 to Apple Silicon / Metal 4 game porting runtime and toolkit"
publish = false

[workspace.dependencies]
win64-abi = { path = "crates/win64-abi" }
bink2-codec = { path = "crates/bink2-codec" }
aw2-data = { path = "games/alan-wake-2/crates/aw2-data" }
# default-features = false: the loader-side D3D12 layer must not pull in the CLI/aw2-data
# (`tools`); tools that want it (the CLI itself) enable it explicitly.
dxil-metal = { path = "crates/dxil-metal", default-features = false }
pe-image = { path = "crates/pe-image" }
port-manifest = { path = "crates/port-manifest" }
port-factory = { path = "crates/port-factory" }
win32-rt = { path = "crates/win32-rt" }
x86-runtime = { path = "crates/x86-runtime" }
aw2-game = { path = "games/alan-wake-2/crates/aw2-game", default-features = false }
d3d12-metal = { path = "crates/d3d12-metal" }
wwise-audio = { path = "crates/wwise-audio" }
goblin = "0.9"
iced-x86 = { version = "1.21", default-features = false, features = ["std", "decoder", "encoder", "block_encoder", "instr_info", "intel"] }
naga = { version = "30.0.1", default-features = false, features = ["spv-in", "wgsl-in", "msl-out"] }
libc = "0.2"
log = "0.4"
env_logger = "0.11"
anyhow = "1"
# Address-keyed park/unpark: implements CRITICAL_SECTION / SRWLOCK / CONDITION_VARIABLE /
# WaitOnAddress whose state must live inside Windows-owned memory (win32-rt).
parking_lot_core = "0.9"
# Client-side TLS 1.2 / 1.3 engine for Windows Schannel (secur32.dll) with embedded WebPKI roots
# (zero external dylibs, 100% SSD-portable across Apple Silicon Macs).
rustls = { version = "0.23", default-features = false, features = ["ring", "std", "tls12"] }
webpki-roots = "1"
# Vendored BSD-3-Clause Meta Zstandard C library for zstd.dll / libzstd.dll
# (zero external dylibs, works on both macOS and iOS).
zstd-sys = { version = "2", default-features = false, features = ["std"] }

[profile.dev]
opt-level = 1
panic = "abort"
incremental = false

[profile.dev.package.aw2-game]
# With all 8 guest DLLs lifted full-image alongside AlanWake2.exe (~353k entries),
# opt-level = 0 produces a ~2.0 GiB Mach-O (__text 1.93 GiB, __eh_frame > 16 MiB),
# hitting macOS's 2 GiB CS_CodeDirectory limit. opt-level = 1 across 256 CGUs keeps
# the binary ~550 MiB and __eh_frame compact.
opt-level = 1
debug = 0
codegen-units = 256

[profile.test]
incremental = false

[profile.test.package.aw2-game]
opt-level = 1
debug = 0
codegen-units = 256

[profile.release]
debug = 1
panic = "abort"

[profile.release.package.aw2-game]
# aw2-game holds the emulator/JIT runtime, the hand-written ns replacements and the
# lifted shards. At opt-level 0 trivial helpers (f32::from_bits, read_unaligned,
# Range::next) stay real calls: gameplay ran at ~11-15 fps. opt-level 2 with 256
# codegen units builds in ~8.5 min (vs ~6 min) and runs gameplay at ~50 fps.
opt-level = 2
debug = 0
codegen-units = 256

FILE: CONTRIBUTING.md
================================================
# Contributing to Apple Silicon Port Factory

Thank you for your interest in contributing! This is an **experimental** project dedicated to
running legitimate Windows x86-64 games that users already legally own on Apple Silicon (macOS and
iOS) via Metal 4, MetalFX, and native ARM64 translation.

Please read [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) alongside this guide for detailed crate boundaries, build
commands, and repo conventions.

---

## Ground Rules

1. **Never commit copyrighted game files, binaries, assets, or captures.**
   No `.exe`, `.dll`, `.rmdblob`, `.rmdtoc`, `.binrfx`, `.bnk`, `.wem`, `.bk2`, `.pak`, `.ucas`,
   `.utoc`, `.app/`, `.ipa`, game screenshots, or Steam library HTML dumps.
2. **Never hardcode personal machine paths.**
   No `/Users/<name>`, personal `/Volumes/<drive>`, or launcher-specific personal directories in
   code, tests, scripts, or docs. Always read game locations from CLI flags (`--game-dir`, `--pe`)
   or environment variables (`PORT_GAME_DIR`, `AW2_GAME_DIR`), and make tests that depend on a local
   game install skip cleanly when the env var is unset.
3. **Keep `crates/` completely game-agnostic.**
   Do not add game-specific virtual addresses, hardcoded game names, or engine-specific hacks to
   `crates/`. Configure per-game behavior declaratively in `games/<id>/game.toml` or via universal
   traits and configuration (`AppConfig`, `DllRegistry`, `ComRegistry`, `x86_runtime::hooks`).
4. **No anti-cheat or DRM bypasses.**
   Anti-cheat and DRM requirements must be detected and reported honestly. PRs that attempt to
   circumvent kernel anti-cheat or DRM protections will not be accepted.
5. **Be honest about stubs and errors.**
   Never fake success or silently `memcpy` past an unimplemented API. Unimplemented exports should
   log via `stub!` / `failing!` and return the documented Windows failure code.
6. **Keep Metal calls inside `d3d12-metal` / `dxil-metal`.**
   Never import `objc2-metal` inside `crates/win32-rt`.

---

## Adding a New Game Target

Most games need **zero custom Rust code** — just a declarative manifest plus any genuinely missing
Win32, x86-64, or graphics fixes in the universal crates:

1. **Audit your legally owned Windows install:**
   ```sh
   cargo opsdb -- scan /path/to/Game
   cargo opsdb -- win32-audit /path/to/Game
   cargo opsdb -- anticheat-audit /path/to/Game/Game.exe
   cargo opsdb -- app-audit /path/to/Game/Game.exe
   cargo opsdb -- runtime-audit /path/to/Game
   ```
2. **Create a manifest from the starter template:**
   ```sh
   mkdir -p games/<game-id>
   cp games/_template/game.toml games/<game-id>/game.toml
   ```
   Edit `games/<game-id>/game.toml` with the game's `id`, `name`, `exe`, virtual `win_dir`,
   optional `steam_app_id`, and runtime flags (`builtin_overrides`, `args`, etc.).
3. **Prewarm the ARM64 `.aot` block cache and test booting:**
   ```sh
   cargo runner-build
   cargo runner -- --manifest games/<game-id>/game.toml --game-dir /path/to/Game --prewarm-aot
   cargo runner -- --manifest games/<game-id>/game.toml --game-dir /path/to/Game
   ```
4. **Fix universal blockers (when encountered):**
   - **Missing Win32/CRT/COM export (`UNIMPLEMENTED foo.dll!Bar`):**
     Implement it in `crates/win32-rt/src/` (or scaffold a new DLL module with
     `cargo opsdb -- scaffold-dll`), add a unit test, and run:
     ```sh
     cargo opsdb -- win32-sync
     ```
     Commit the updated `db/win32_apis.tsv` with your change (it is plain text, so the diff
     shows exactly which exports were added).
   - **Missing or buggy x86-64 instruction:**
     Implement it in `crates/x86-runtime/`, add unit/differential tests, and sync `db/ops_x86_to_arm64.tsv`
     if you added a new catalog entry (`cargo opsdb -- sync`).
   - **D3D12 / D3D11 / Vulkan / shader bug:**
     Fix it in `crates/d3d12-metal/`, `crates/win32-rt/src/d3d11/`, `crates/win32-rt/src/misc/vulkan_1.rs`,
     or `crates/dxil-metal/` with a regression test.
5. **Document test results honestly in your PR** (what boots/renders, what still fails, macOS
   version, Apple Silicon chip, and what tests you ran).

---

## Before Opening a Pull Request

Run the relevant checks and confirm zero compiler warnings in files you touched:

```sh
# 1. Universal runner builds cleanly (~15s)
cargo runner-build

# 2. Universal test suite passes
cargo factory-test -- --test-threads=1

# 3. Catalog TSVs are in sync with source code
cargo opsdb -- doctor

# 4. If you edited graphics, Win32, or shared traits:
cargo test -p d3d12-metal --lib --target aarch64-apple-darwin -- --test-threads=1
cargo test -p win32-rt --lib --target aarch64-apple-darwin -- d3d11 misc::vulkan_1 --test-threads=1
cargo check -p aw2-game --no-default-features --target aarch64-apple-darwin
```

The headless Win32 corpus builds its Windows x86-64 fixtures with Zig 0.16.0. Install that
version on your PATH or set `ZIG` to its executable, then run
`cargo test -p port-loader --target aarch64-apple-darwin --test os_guest_corpus -- --test-threads=1`.
Each guest must pass with both the live JIT and its prewarmed AOT cache.

Continuous integration (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) runs the repo hygiene audit, checks 1–3 above,
the headless Win32 corpus, the AOT multi-module verification test, the `aw2-game` check and an iOS library check on every
pull request. It runs on GitHub-hosted runners without a physical GPU, so the Metal 4, audio and
end-to-end tests in step 4 still need to be run locally on Apple Silicon hardware.

Use small, focused commits following `<area>: <imperative summary>` (for example:
`win32: implement GetFileInformationByHandleEx`, `x86-runtime: lower vpermq in ARM64 JIT`,
`games: add manifest for <title>`).

[package]
name = "bink2-codec"
description = "Clean-room native Rust Bink 2 (KB2f..KB2n) decoder and hardware HEVC (KB2v) container codec"
edition.workspace = true

================================================
FILE: crates/bink2-codec/src/codec.rs
================================================
//! Clean-room native ARM64/Rust Bink 2 (`KB2f`..`KB2n`, integer path) video decoder.
//!
//! Decodes multi-slice macroblock frames (32x32 luma/alpha/HDR, 16x16 Cr/Cb) into
//! caller-registered planar frame buffers (`Y`, `cR`, `cB`, optional `A`, optional `H`).

use super::dsp::{
    add_clamp_16x8, avg_16x8_pavgb, avg_32x8_pavgb, avg_8x8_pavgb, clamp_store_16x8, copy_16x16,
    copy_16x8, deblock_horiz_16, deblock_vert_8, idct8x8, idct8x8_dc, mc_chroma_8x8, mc_luma_16x16,
    smooth_dc_pair, smooth_inter_horiz, smooth_inter_vert,
};
use super::tables::{
    AC_COUNT, AC_RUN, AC_VLC1, AC_VLC2, CBP4_DEFAULT, DC_QUANT, DEBLOCK_LUT_A, DEBLOCK_LUT_B,
    DEBLOCK_LUT_C, MV_CLAMP, MV_VLC, QDELTA_LUT, QMAT_INTER, QMAT_INTRA_CHROMA, QMAT_INTRA_LUMA,
    SLICE_COUNT, ZIGZAG_U8,
};

/// Planar destination/reference view for one Bink 2 frame decode.
pub struct FramePlanes<'a> {
    pub ya_width: usize,
    pub ya_height: usize,
    pub ya_pitch: usize,
    pub crcb_pitch: usize,
    pub dst_y: &'a mut [u8],
    pub dst_cr: &'a mut [u8],
    pub dst_cb: &'a mut [u8],
    pub dst_a: Option<&'a mut [u8]>,
    pub dst_h: Option<&'a mut [u8]>,
    pub ref_y: &'a [u8],
    pub ref_cr: &'a [u8],
    pub ref_cb: &'a [u8],
    pub ref_a: Option<&'a [u8]>,
    pub ref_h: Option<&'a [u8]>,
}

/// Slice partitioning for a Bink 2 video stream (`compute_slices`).
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct SliceInfo {
    pub num_slices: u32,
    pub slice_ends: [u32; 8],
}

/// Compute slice count and per-slice ending Y rows (multiples of 32) from the
/// Bink fourCC, open flags, and display dimensions.
pub fn compute_slices(fourcc: u32, open_flags: u32, _width: u32, height: u32) -> SliceInfo {
    let mut info = SliceInfo {
        num_slices: 1,
        slice_ends: [0; 8],
    };
    let kb2f = u32::from_le_bytes(*b"KB2f");
    let kb2g = u32::from_le_bytes(*b"KB2g");
    if fourcc == kb2f {
        info.num_slices = 2;
        let mid = ((height.wrapping_add(32)) >> 1) & !31;
        info.slice_ends[0] = mid;
        if mid != height {
            info.slice_ends[1] = height;
        }
        return info;
    }
    let h_pad = (height.wrapping_add(31)) & !31;
    if fourcc == kb2g {
        if h_pad >= 128 {
            info.num_slices = 2;
            info.slice_ends[0] = (h_pad >> 1) & !31;
            info.slice_ends[1] = h_pad;
            return info;
        }
        info.num_slices = 1;
        info.slice_ends[0] = h_pad;
        return info;
    }
    if h_pad >= 128 {
        let n = SLICE_COUNT[(open_flags & 3) as usize];
        info.num_slices = n;
        if n == 1 {
            info.slice_ends[0] = h_pad;
            return info;
        }
        let mut cur = ((n * 32 - 1 + h_pad) / n) & !31;
        info.slice_ends[0] = cur;
        let mut rem = n - 1;
        if rem == 1 {
            info.slice_ends[1] = h_pad;
            return info;
        }
        let mut idx = 1usize;
        while rem > 1 {
            let step = ((rem * 32 - 1 + h_pad - cur) / rem) & !31;
            cur += step;
            info.slice_ends[idx] = cur;
            idx += 1;
            rem -= 1;
        }
        info.slice_ends[idx] = h_pad;
        return info;
    }
    info.num_slices = 1;
    info.slice_ends[0] = h_pad;
    info
}

#[cold]
#[inline(never)]
fn read_tail_word(data: &[u8], off: usize) -> u32 {
    if off < data.len() {
        let mut b = [0u8; 4];
        let rem = data.len() - off;
        b[..rem].copy_from_slice(&data[off..]);
        u32::from_le_bytes(b)
    } else {
        0
    }
}

/// LSB-first 64-bit buffered word-oriented bitstream reader matching Bink 2's reader layout.
pub struct BitReader<'a> {
    data: &'a [u8],
    base_offset: usize,
    next_word_idx: usize,
    bit_buf: u64,
    bits_left: u32,
}

impl<'a> BitReader<'a> {
    pub fn new(data: &'a [u8], byte_offset: usize) -> Self {
        let slice = if byte_offset <= data.len() {
            &data[byte_offset..]
        } else {
            &[]
        };
        Self {
            data: slice,
            base_offset: byte_offset,
            next_word_idx: 0,
            bit_buf: 0,
            bits_left: 0,
        }
    }

    #[inline(always)]
    fn word_at(&self, widx: usize) -> u32 {
        let off = widx << 2;
        if off + 4 <= self.data.len() {
            // SAFETY: `off + 4 <= self.data.len()` guarantees 4 valid readable bytes.
            unsafe {
                let ptr = self.data.as_ptr().add(off) as *const [u8; 4];
                u32::from_le_bytes(std::ptr::read_unaligned(ptr))
            }
        } else {
            read_tail_word(self.data, off)
        }
    }

    /// Ensure at least 32 valid bits are buffered in `bit_buf`.
    #[inline(always)]
    fn refill(&mut self) {
        if self.bits_left < 32 {
            let w = self.word_at(self.next_word_idx) as u64;
            self.bit_buf |= w << self.bits_left;
            self.next_word_idx += 1;
            self.bits_left += 32;
        }
    }

    #[inline]
    pub fn byte_pos(&self) -> usize {
        let unread_words = (self.bits_left >> 5) as usize;
        self.base_offset + (self.next_word_idx - unread_words) * 4
    }

    #[inline]
    pub fn align_u32(&mut self) {
        let rem = self.bits_left & 31;
        if rem != 0 {
            self.bit_buf >>= rem;
            self.bits_left -= rem;
        }
    }

    #[inline(always)]
    pub fn read_bit(&mut self) -> u32 {
        if self.bits_left == 0 {
            let w = self.word_at(self.next_word_idx) as u64;
            self.next_word_idx += 1;
            self.bit_buf = w >> 1;
            self.bits_left = 31;
            (w as u32) & 1
        } else {
            let b = (self.bit_buf as u32) & 1;
            self.bit_buf >>= 1;
            self.bits_left -= 1;
            b
        }
    }

    #[inline(always)]
    pub fn read_bits(&mut self, n: u32) -> u32 {
        if n == 0 {
            return 0;
        }
        if self.bits_left < n {
            let w = self.word_at(self.next_word_idx) as u64;
            self.bit_buf |= w << self.bits_left;
            self.next_word_idx += 1;
            self.bits_left += 32;
        }
        let v = self.bit_buf as u32;
        self.bit_buf >>= n;
        self.bits_left -= n;
        let mask = if n >= 32 { u32::MAX } else { (1u32 << n) - 1 };
        v & mask
    }

    #[inline(always)]
    pub fn peek_bits(&self, n: u32) -> u32 {
        if n == 0 {
            return 0;
        }
        let mut v = self.bit_buf;
        if self.bits_left < n {
            let next = self.word_at(self.next_word_idx) as u64;
            v |= next << self.bits_left;
        }
        let mask = if n >= 32 { u32::MAX } else { (1u32 << n) - 1 };
        (v as u32) & mask
    }

    #[inline(always)]
    pub fn skip_bits(&mut self, n: u32) {
        if n == 0 {
            return;
        }
        if self.bits_left < n {
            let w = self.word_at(self.next_word_idx) as u64;
            self.bit_buf |= w << self.bits_left;
            self.next_word_idx += 1;
            self.bits_left += 32;
        }
        self.bit_buf >>= n;
        self.bits_left -= n;
    }

    /// Consume `n` bits (`n <= 32`) when caller already ensured `bits_left >= n` via `refill()`.
    #[inline(always)]
    fn consume_fast(&mut self, n: u32) {
        self.bit_buf >>= n;
        self.bits_left -= n;
    }
}

/// Decode a row-skip or column-skip deblock bitmap (`180022680`).
fn decode_rle_bitmap(out: &mut [u8], mut bit_pos: u32, bs: &mut BitReader, mut count: u32) {
    out.fill(0);
    if count == 0 {
        return;
    }
    let mut byte_idx = 0usize;
    let mut acc = 0u32;

    if bs.read_bit() == 0 {
        while count > 8 {
            let b = bs.read_bits(8);
            acc |= b << bit_pos;
            let next_pos = bit_pos + 8;
            if next_pos > 7 {
                if byte_idx < out.len() {
                    out[byte_idx] = acc as u8;
                }
                byte_idx += 1;
                acc >>= 8;
                bit_pos = next_pos - 8;
            } else {
                bit_pos = next_pos;
            }
            count -= 8;
        }
        if count != 0 {
            let b = bs.read_bits(count);
            acc |= b << bit_pos;
            if byte_idx < out.len() {
                out[byte_idx] = acc as u8;
            }
        }
        return;
    }

    let mut cur_bit = 0u32;
    let mut state = 0i32;

    while count != 0 {
        if bs.read_bit() == 0 {
            if state == 3 {
                cur_bit ^= 1;
            } else {
                cur_bit = bs.read_bit();
            }
            state = 2;
            let rem = count - 1;
            if rem < 4 {
                let extra = bs.read_bits(rem);
                acc |= (cur_bit << bit_pos) | (extra << (bit_pos + 1));
                bit_pos += 1 + rem;
                count = 0;
            } else {
                let extra = bs.read_bits(4);
                acc |= (cur_bit << bit_pos) | (extra << (bit_pos + 1));
                bit_pos += 5;
                count -= 5;
            }
            if bit_pos > 7 {
                if byte_idx < out.len() {
                    out[byte_idx] = acc as u8;
                }
                byte_idx += 1;
                acc >>= 8;
                bit_pos -= 8;
            }
        } else {
            let len_bits = if count < 4 {
                2u32
            } else if count >= 8 && count >= 16 {
                5u32
            } else if count >= 8 {
                4u32
            } else {
                4u32
            };
            let mut run = if state == 3 {
                cur_bit ^= 1;
                len_bits + 1
            } else {
                cur_bit = bs.read_bit();
                len_bits + 2
            };
            if count < run {
                run = count;
            }
            count -= run;
            if count != 0 {
                let extra = bs.read_bits(len_bits);
                state = if extra == (1u32 << len_bits) - 1 {
                    1
                } else {
                    3
                };
                run += extra;
                count = count.saturating_sub(extra);
            }
            let fill_byte = if cur_bit != 0 { 0xffu32 } else { 0u32 };
            while run > 8 {
                acc |= fill_byte << bit_pos;
                if byte_idx < out.len() {
                    out[byte_idx] = acc as u8;
                }
                byte_idx += 1;
                acc >>= 8;
                run -= 8;
            }
            if run != 0 {
                let mask = (1u32 << run) - 1;
                acc |= (fill_byte & mask) << bit_pos;
                bit_pos += run;
                if bit_pos > 7 {
                    if byte_idx < out.len() {
                        out[byte_idx] = acc as u8;
                    }
                    byte_idx += 1;
                    acc >>= 8;
                    bit_pos -= 8;
                }
            }
        }
    }
    if bit_pos != 0 && byte_idx < out.len() {
        out[byte_idx] = acc as u8;
    }
}

/// Move-to-front macroblock mode decoder (`180022ac0`).
#[inline]
fn decode_mode_mtf(bs: &mut BitReader, mtf: &mut [u8; 4]) -> u8 {
    bs.refill();
    let v = (bs.bit_buf as u32) & 7;
    if (v & 1) != 0 {
        bs.consume_fast(1);
        mtf[0]
    } else if (v & 2) != 0 {
        bs.consume_fast(2);
        mtf.swap(0, 1);
        mtf[0]
    } else if v == 0 {
        bs.consume_fast(3);
        mtf.swap(1, 2);
        mtf[1]
    } else {
        bs.consume_fast(3);
        mtf.swap(2, 3);
        mtf[2]
    }
}

/// Decode quantizer delta (`180014ae0`).
#[inline]
fn decode_qdelta(bs: &mut BitReader, prev_q: i32) -> i32 {
    bs.refill();
    let v = (bs.bit_buf as u32) & 0x1ff;
    let mut mag: i32 = if (v & 7) != 0 {
        let m = QDELTA_LUT[(v & 7) as usize] as i32;
        bs.consume_fast((m + 1) as u32);
        m
    } else if (v & 0xf) == 0 {
        bs.consume_fast(9);
        ((v >> 4) + 5) as i32
    } else {
        bs.consume_fast(5);
        (((v >> 4) & 1) + 3) as i32
    };
    if mag != 0 {
        let sign = (bs.bit_buf as u32) & 1;
        bs.consume_fast(1);
        if sign != 0 {
            mag = -mag;
        }
    }
    prev_q.wrapping_add(mag)
}

#[inline]
fn median3_i32(a: i32, b: i32, c: i32) -> i32 {
    let (lo, hi) = if a < b { (a, b) } else { (b, a) };
    c.clamp(lo, hi)
}

#[inline]
fn median3_u8(a: u8, b: u8, c: u8) -> u8 {
    let (lo, hi) = if a < b { (a, b) } else { (b, a) };
    c.clamp(lo, hi)
}

#[inline]
fn pred_grad(a: i16, b: i16, c: i16) -> i16 {
    let mn = a.min(b).min(c);
    let mx = a.max(b).max(c);
    a.wrapping_add(b).wrapping_sub(c).clamp(mn, mx)
}

/// Decode 16-block coded-block-pattern (`1800147b0`).
fn decode_cbp16(bs: &mut BitReader, prev: u32, flags: u32) -> u32 {
    let mut cnt = (prev & 0xffff).count_ones();
    let invert = if cnt > 7 {
        cnt = 16 - cnt;
        0xffffu32
    } else {
        0u32
    };
    let mut cbp = 0u32;
    if bs.read_bit() == 0 {
        if cnt < 4 {
            for _ in 0..4 {
                cbp >>= 4;
                if bs.read_bit() == 0 {
                    cbp |= bs.read_bits(4) << 12;
                }
            }
        } else {
            cbp = bs.read_bits(16);
        }
    }
    cbp ^= invert;
    if ((flags >> 18) & 1) == 0 || (cbp & 0xffff) != 0 {
        if bs.read_bit() != 0 {
            cbp |= cbp << 16;
        }
    }
    cbp
}

/// Decode 4-block coded-block-pattern (`1800149e0`).
fn decode_cbp4(bs: &mut BitReader, prev: u32) -> u32 {
    let def = CBP4_DEFAULT[(prev & 0xf) as usize];
    if bs.read_bit() != 0 {
        return def | (prev & 0xf_0000);
    }
    let mut cbp = bs.read_bits(4);
    if bs.read_bit() != 0 {
        cbp |= cbp << 16;
    }
    cbp
}

/// Decode `N` (4 or 16) dequantized DC deltas (`180012cd0` / `180013af0`).
fn decode_dc_deltas<const N: usize>(bs: &mut BitReader, q: u32) -> ([i16; N], bool) {
    let mut out = [0i16; N];
    if bs.read_bit() == 0 {
        return (out, false);
    }
    let q_idx = (q.max(8) as usize).min(DC_QUANT.len() - 1);
    let scale = DC_QUANT[q_idx] as i32;
    for slot in &mut out {
        bs.refill();
        let peek = (bs.bit_buf as u32) & 0x7ff;
        let k = (!peek).trailing_zeros();
        bs.consume_fast(k + 1);
        if k != 0 {
            let level = if k > 3 {
                let eb = k - 3;
                let extra = (bs.bit_buf as u32) & ((1u32 << eb) - 1);
                bs.consume_fast(eb);
                (extra + 2 + (1u32 << eb)) as i32
            } else {
                k as i32
            };
            let sign = (bs.bit_buf as u32) & 1;
            bs.consume_fast(1);
            let mag = (level.wrapping_mul(scale).wrapping_add(0x200)) >> 10;
            *slot = if sign != 0 { -mag } else { mag } as i16;
        }
    }
    (out, true)
}

/// Decode 16-block DC grid in 4x4 Morton quad order (`180012e70`).
///
/// `dc` holds the left MB's 16 DCs on entry (indices `5, 7, 13, 15` are left edge)
/// and receives the current MB's 16 DCs on exit.
/// `above` holds `[_, _, _, above_left, above0, above1, above2, above3]` if `Some`,
/// or `None` for inter residuals (initial DC prediction = 0).
fn decode_dc16(
    dc: &mut [i32; 16],
    above: Option<&[i32; 8]>,
    q: u32,
    bs: &mut BitReader,
    flags: u32,
    min_dc: i16,
    max_dc: i16,
) {
    let (deltas, nonzero) = decode_dc_deltas::<16>(bs, q);
    if !nonzero && above.is_none() && (flags & 0xa8) == 0xa8 {
        dc.fill(0);
        return;
    }

    let left_edge = (flags & 0x20) != 0;
    let top_edge = (flags & 0x88) != 0;

    let l0 = dc[5] as i16;
    let l1 = dc[7] as i16;
    let l2 = dc[13] as i16;
    let l3 = dc[15] as i16;

    let (al, a0, a1, a2, a3) = match above {
        Some(ab) => (
            ab[3] as i16,
            ab[4] as i16,
            ab[5] as i16,
            ab[6] as i16,
            ab[7] as i16,
        ),
        None => (0, 0, 0, 0, 0),
    };

    let mut v = [0i16; 16];

    // Quad 0 (top-left): blocks 0, 1, 2, 3
    let p0 = match (left_edge, top_edge) {
        (false, false) => pred_grad(l0, a0, al),
        (false, true) => l0,
        (true, false) => a0,
        (true, true) => {
            if above.is_some() {
                0x400
            } else {
                0
            }
        }
    };
    v[0] = p0.wrapping_add(deltas[0]).clamp(min_dc, max_dc);

    let p1 = if !top_edge {
        pred_grad(v[0], a1, a0)
    } else {
        v[0]
    };
    v[1] = p1.wrapping_add(deltas[1]).clamp(min_dc, max_dc);

    let p2 = if !left_edge {
        pred_grad(v[0], l1, l0)
    } else {
        v[0]
    };
    v[2] = p2.wrapping_add(deltas[2]).clamp(min_dc, max_dc);

    v[3] = pred_grad(v[1], v[2], v[0])
        .wrapping_add(deltas[3])
        .clamp(min_dc, max_dc);

    // Quad 1 (top-right): blocks 4, 5, 6, 7
    let p4 = if !top_edge {
        pred_grad(v[1], a2, a1)
    } else {
        v[1]
    };
    v[4] = p4.wrapping_add(deltas[4]).clamp(min_dc, max_dc);

    let p5 = if !top_edge {
        pred_grad(v[4], a3, a2)
    } else {
        v[4]
    };
    v[5] = p5.wrapping_add(deltas[5]).clamp(min_dc, max_dc);

    v[6] = pred_grad(v[4], v[3], v[1])
        .wrapping_add(deltas[6])
        .clamp(min_dc, max_dc);
    v[7] = pred_grad(v[5], v[6], v[4])
        .wrapping_add(deltas[7])
        .clamp(min_dc, max_dc);

    // Quad 2 (bottom-left): blocks 8, 9, 10, 11
    let p8 = if !left_edge {
        pred_grad(v[2], l2, l1)
    } else {
        v[2]
    };
    v[8] = p8.wrapping_add(deltas[8]).clamp(min_dc, max_dc);

    v[9] = pred_grad(v[3], v[8], v[2])
        .wrapping_add(deltas[9])
        .clamp(min_dc, max_dc);

    let p10 = if !left_edge {
        pred_grad(v[8], l3, l2)
    } else {
        v[8]
    };
    v[10] = p10.wrapping_add(deltas[10]).clamp(min_dc, max_dc);

    v[11] = pred_grad(v[9], v[10], v[8])
        .wrapping_add(deltas[11])
        .clamp(min_dc, max_dc);

    // Quad 3 (bottom-right): blocks 12, 13, 14, 15
    v[12] = pred_grad(v[6], v[9], v[3])
        .wrapping_add(deltas[12])
        .clamp(min_dc, max_dc);
    v[13] = pred_grad(v[7], v[12], v[6])
        .wrapping_add(deltas[13])
        .clamp(min_dc, max_dc);
    v[14] = pred_grad(v[12], v[11], v[9])
        .wrapping_add(deltas[14])
        .clamp(min_dc, max_dc);
    v[15] = pred_grad(v[13], v[14], v[12])
        .wrapping_add(deltas[15])
        .clamp(min_dc, max_dc);

    for i in 0..16 {
        dc[i] = v[i] as i32;
    }
}

/// Decode 4-block chroma DC grid (`180013c80`).
fn decode_dc4(
    dc: &mut [i32; 4],
    above: Option<&[i32; 4]>,
    q: u32,
    bs: &mut BitReader,
    flags: u32,
    min_dc: i16,
    max_dc: i16,
) {
    let (deltas, _nonzero) = decode_dc_deltas::<4>(bs, q);
    let left_edge = (flags & 0x20) != 0;
    let top_edge = (flags & 0x88) != 0;

    let l0 = dc[1] as i16;
    let l1 = dc[3] as i16;
    let (al, a0, a1) = match above {
        Some(ab) => (ab[1] as i16, ab[2] as i16, ab[3] as i16),
        None => (0, 0, 0),
    };

    let p0 = match (left_edge, top_edge) {
        (false, false) => pred_grad(l0, a0, al),
        (false, true) => l0,
        (true, false) => a0,
        (true, true) => {
            if above.is_some() {
                0x400
            } else {
                0
            }
        }
    };
    let v0 = p0.wrapping_add(deltas[0]).clamp(min_dc, max_dc);
    let p1 = if !top_edge {
        pred_grad(v0, a1, a0)
    } else {
        v0
    };
    let v1 = p1.wrapping_add(deltas[1]).clamp(min_dc, max_dc);
    let p2 = if !left_edge {
        pred_grad(v0, l1, l0)
    } else {
        v0
    };
    let v2 = p2.wrapping_add(deltas[2]).clamp(min_dc, max_dc);
    let v3 = pred_grad(v1, v2, v0)
        .wrapping_add(deltas[3])
        .clamp(min_dc, max_dc);

    dc[0] = v0 as i32;
    dc[1] = v1 as i32;
    dc[2] = v2 as i32;
    dc[3] = v3 as i32;
}

#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
#[inline(always)]
fn decode_ac_quad_inner<const USE_ALT: bool>(
    mut cbp_bits: u32,
    coeffs: &mut [i16; 256],
    bs: &mut BitReader,
    q: u32,
    qmat: &[[u16; 64]; 4],
    dcs: &[i32; 4],
    info_out: &mut [u16; 4],
    base_flag: u16,
) -> u32 {
    let q_phase = (q & 3) as usize;
    let q_shift = (q >> 2) & 31;
    let qm = &qmat[q_phase];
    let mut last_nz_packed = 0u32;

    for b in 0..4 {
        let blk = &mut coeffs[b * 64..(b + 1) * 64];
        let mut nz_count = 0u32;
        let mut last_nz = 0u32;
        if (cbp_bits & 1) != 0 {
            blk.fill(0);
            let mut rem_nz = 0i32;
            let mut idx = 1usize;
            while idx < 64 {
                rem_nz -= 1;
                let pos = if rem_nz < 1 {
                    bs.refill();
                    let entry = if USE_ALT {
                        AC_VLC2[(bs.bit_buf as usize) & 0xff]
                    } else {
                        AC_VLC1[(bs.bit_buf as usize) & 0x1ff]
                    };
                    let sym = ((entry >> 4) & 0xf) as usize;
                    let blen = (entry & 0xf) as u32;
                    bs.consume_fast(blen);
                    let mut run = AC_RUN[sym];
                    rem_nz = AC_COUNT[sym] as i32;
                    if run == 11 {
                        run = (bs.bit_buf as usize) & 0x3f;
                        bs.consume_fast(6);
                    }
                    let p = idx + run;
                    if p >= 64 {
                        break;
                    }
                    p
                } else {
                    idx
                };

                bs.refill();
                let peek = (bs.bit_buf as u32) & 0x7ff;
                let k = (!peek).trailing_zeros();
                let n = k + 1;
                bs.consume_fast(n);
                let mag = if n > 3 {
                    let eb = k - 2;
                    let extra = (bs.bit_buf as u32) & ((1u32 << eb) - 1);
                    bs.consume_fast(eb);
                    extra + 2 + (1u32 << eb)
                } else {
                    n
                };
                let sign = (bs.bit_buf as u32) & 1;
                bs.consume_fast(1);
                let signed = if sign != 0 { -(mag as i32) } else { mag as i32 };
                let zz = (ZIGZAG_U8[pos] as usize) & 63;
                let deq = ((signed.wrapping_mul(qm[zz] as i32)) << q_shift).wrapping_add(0x40) >> 7;
                blk[zz] = deq as i16;
                nz_count += 1;
                last_nz = pos as u32;
                idx = pos + 1;
            }
        }
        let dc = dcs[b] as u16;
        blk[0] = dc.wrapping_add(4).wrapping_mul(8) as i16;
        last_nz_packed = (last_nz << 24) | (last_nz_packed >> 8);
        let sp =
            (u16::from(nz_count < 4) + u16::from(nz_count < 8) + u16::from(nz_count == 0)) * 0x2000;
        info_out[b] = sp | (dc & 0x1fff) | base_flag;
        cbp_bits = (cbp_bits >> 1) & 0x7fff_ffff;
    }

    last_nz_packed
}

/// Decode AC coefficients for a 4-block (16x16) quad (`1800123a0`).
///
/// Returns packed 4 bytes of `last_nz` (byte `b` = last nonzero zigzag index in block `b`,
/// or `0` if DC-only).
#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
fn decode_ac_quad(
    cbp_bits: u32,
    coeffs: &mut [i16; 256],
    bs: &mut BitReader,
    q: u32,
    qmat: &[[u16; 64]; 4],
    dcs: &[i32; 4],
    info_out: &mut [u16; 4],
    base_flag: u16,
) -> u32 {
    if (cbp_bits & 0xf) == 0 {
        for b in 0..4 {
            let dc = dcs[b] as u16;
            coeffs[b * 64] = dc.wrapping_add(4).wrapping_mul(8) as i16;
            info_out[b] = (dc & 0x1fff) | base_flag | 0x6000;
        }
        return 0;
    }

    if (cbp_bits & 0xffff_0000) != 0 {
        decode_ac_quad_inner::<true>(cbp_bits, coeffs, bs, q, qmat, dcs, info_out, base_flag)
    } else {
        decode_ac_quad_inner::<false>(cbp_bits, coeffs, bs, q, qmat, dcs, info_out, base_flag)
    }
}

/// Transform a 4-block (16x16) coefficient quad into a 16x16 `i16` buffer (stride `out_stride` in `i16`s).
#[inline]
fn idct_quad(
    coeffs: &[i16; 256],
    mut last_nz_packed: u32,
    out: &mut [i16],
    out_off: usize,
    out_stride: usize,
) {
    let offsets = [0usize, 8, out_stride * 8, out_stride * 8 + 8];
    for b in 0..4 {
        let blk: &[i16; 64] = coeffs[b * 64..(b + 1) * 64].try_into().unwrap();
        let off = out_off + offsets[b];
        if (last_nz_packed & 0xff) == 0 {
            idct8x8_dc(blk[0], out, off, out_stride);
        } else {
            idct8x8(blk, out, off, out_stride);
        }
        last_nz_packed >>= 8;
    }
}

/// Decode a 32x32 intra plane (Y, A, or H) (`180014c80`).
#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
fn decode_intra_plane16(
    dc: &mut [i32; 16],
    above: &[i32; 8],
    mut dst: Option<(&mut [u8], usize, usize)>,
    bs: &mut BitReader,
    info_out: &mut [u16; 16],
    flags: u32,
    q: u32,
    cbp_state: &mut u32,
) {
    let mut cbp = decode_cbp16(bs, *cbp_state, flags);
    *cbp_state = cbp;
    decode_dc16(dc, Some(above), q, bs, flags, 0, 0x7ff);

    let mut coeffs = [0i16; 256];
    let mut tmp = [0i16; 256];
    for quad in 0..4usize {
        let dcs = [
            dc[quad * 4],
            dc[quad * 4 + 1],
            dc[quad * 4 + 2],
            dc[quad * 4 + 3],
        ];
        let mut qinfo = [0u16; 4];
        let last_nz = decode_ac_quad(
            cbp,
            &mut coeffs,
            bs,
            q,
            &QMAT_INTRA_LUMA,
            &dcs,
            &mut qinfo,
            0x8000,
        );
        info_out[quad * 4..quad * 4 + 4].copy_from_slice(&qinfo);
        cbp >>= 4;

        let is_bot = (quad & 2) != 0;
        let write_this = ((!is_bot && (flags & 4) == 0) || (is_bot && (flags & 3) == 0))
            && last_nz != 0;
        if write_this {
            if let Some((ref mut buf, base_off, stride)) = dst {
                idct_quad(&coeffs, last_nz, &mut tmp, 0, 16);
                let qoff = base_off + (quad & 1) * 16 + if is_bot { stride * 16 } else { 0 };
                clamp_store_16x8(buf, qoff, stride, &tmp, 0, 16);
                clamp_store_16x8(buf, qoff + stride * 8, stride, &tmp, 128, 16);
            }
        }
    }
}

/// Decode a 16x16 intra chroma plane (Cr or Cb) (`180014f00`).
#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
fn decode_intra_plane4(
    dc: &mut [i32; 4],
    above: &[i32; 4],
    dst: &mut [u8],
    dst_off: usize,
    dst_stride: usize,
    bs: &mut BitReader,
    info_out: &mut [u16; 4],
    flags: u32,
    q: u32,
    cbp_state: &mut u32,
) {
    let cbp = decode_cbp4(bs, *cbp_state);
    *cbp_state = cbp;
    decode_dc4(dc, Some(above), q, bs, flags, 0, 0x7ff);

    let mut coeffs = [0i16; 256];
    let last_nz = decode_ac_quad(
        cbp,
        &mut coeffs,
        bs,
        q,
        &QMAT_INTRA_CHROMA,
        dc,
        info_out,
        0x8000,
    );
    if last_nz != 0 {
        let mut tmp = [0i16; 256];
        idct_quad(&coeffs, last_nz, &mut tmp, 0, 16);
        if (flags & 3) == 0 {
            if (flags & 4) == 0 {
                clamp_store_16x8(dst, dst_off, dst_stride, &tmp, 0, 16);
            }
            clamp_store_16x8(dst, dst_off + dst_stride * 8, dst_stride, &tmp, 128, 16);
        } else {
            clamp_store_16x8(dst, dst_off, dst_stride, &tmp, 0, 16);
        }
    }
}

/// Decode a 32x32 inter plane with residual (Y, A, or H) (`1800152e0`).
#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
fn decode_inter_plane16(
    mut dst: Option<(&mut [u8], usize, usize)>,
    ref_plane: Option<(&[u8], usize)>,
    bs: &mut BitReader,
    flags: u32,
    clamped_mvs: &[u32; 8],
    q: u32,
    cbp_state: &mut u32,
    scratch: &mut [u8; 256],
) {
    let mut cbp = decode_cbp16(bs, *cbp_state, flags);
    *cbp_state = cbp;

    let ext_bit = (flags >> 18) & 1;
    let min_dc: i16 = if ext_bit != 0 { -2047 } else { -1023 };
    let max_dc: i16 = if ext_bit != 0 { 2047 } else { 1023 };
    let mut dc = [0i32; 16];
    decode_dc16(&mut dc, None, q, bs, 0xa8, min_dc, max_dc);

    let mut res = [0i16; 1024]; // 32x32 residuals (stride 32)
    let mut info = [0u16; 16];
    let mut coeffs = [0i16; 256];

    for quad in 0..4usize {
        let dcs = [
            dc[quad * 4],
            dc[quad * 4 + 1],
            dc[quad * 4 + 2],
            dc[quad * 4 + 3],
        ];
        let mut qinfo = [0u16; 4];
        let last_nz = decode_ac_quad(
            cbp,
            &mut coeffs,
            bs,
            q,
            &QMAT_INTER,
            &dcs,
            &mut qinfo,
            0,
        );
        info[quad * 4..quad * 4 + 4].copy_from_slice(&qinfo);
        cbp >>= 4;

        if ref_plane.is_some() {
            let qoff = (quad & 2) * 256 + (quad & 1) * 16;
            idct_quad(&coeffs, last_nz, &mut res, qoff, 32);

            if (flags & 0x44000) == 0 {
                if (quad & 1) != 0 {
                    // Boundary between left quad's right column and right quad's left column
                    smooth_inter_horiz(
                        &mut res,
                        qoff - 2,
                        32,
                        info[quad * 4 - 3],
                        info[quad * 4],
                    );
                    smooth_inter_horiz(
                        &mut res,
                        qoff + 256 - 2,
                        32,
                        info[quad * 4 - 1],
                        info[quad * 4 + 2],
                    );
                }
                // Internal vertical boundary inside this 16x16 quad
                smooth_inter_horiz(&mut res, qoff + 6, 32, info[quad * 4], info[quad * 4 + 1]);
                smooth_inter_horiz(
                    &mut res,
                    qoff + 256 + 6,
                    32,
                    info[quad * 4 + 2],
                    info[quad * 4 + 3],
                );
            }
        }
    }

    let Some((ref_buf, ref_stride)) = ref_plane else {
        return;
    };

    if (flags & 0x48000) == 0 {
        for col in 0..4usize {
            let x = col * 8;
            let idx_base = (col & 2) * 2 | (col & 1);
            let i0 = info[idx_base];
            let i1 = info[idx_base + 2];
            let i2 = info[idx_base + 8];
            let i3 = info[idx_base + 10];
            smooth_inter_vert(&mut res, 6 * 32 + x, 32, i0, i1);
            smooth_inter_vert(&mut res, 14 * 32 + x, 32, i1, i2);
            smooth_inter_vert(&mut res, 22 * 32 + x, 32, i2, i3);
        }
    }

    let mut pred = [0u8; 256];
    for quad in 0..4usize {
        let to_scratch = if (flags & 7) == 0 {
            false
        } else if (flags & 4) == 0 {
            if quad == 2 {
                continue;
            }
            quad == 3
        } else {
            if quad == 0 {
                continue;
            }
            quad == 1
        };

        let mx = clamped_mvs[quad * 2];
        let my = clamped_mvs[quad * 2 + 1];
        mc_luma_16x16(
            ref_buf,
            ref_stride,
            (mx >> 1) as i32,
            (my >> 1) as i32,
            &mut pred,
            0,
            16,
            (mx & 1) != 0,
            (my & 1) != 0,
        );
        let roff = (quad & 2) * 256 + (quad & 1) * 16;
        if to_scratch {
            add_clamp_16x8(scratch, 0, 16, &res, roff, 32, &pred, 0, 16);
            add_clamp_16x8(scratch, 128, 16, &res, roff + 256, 32, &pred, 128, 16);
        } else if let Some((ref mut dst_buf, dst_base, dst_stride)) = dst {
            let doff = dst_base
                + (quad & 1) * 16
                + if (quad & 2) != 0 { dst_stride * 16 } else { 0 };
            add_clamp_16x8(dst_buf, doff, dst_stride, &res, roff, 32, &pred, 0, 16);
            add_clamp_16x8(
                dst_buf,
                doff + dst_stride * 8,
                dst_stride,
                &res,
                roff + 256,
                32,
                &pred,
                128,
                16,
            );
        }
    }
}

/// Decode a 16x16 inter chroma plane with residual (Cr or Cb) (`180015830`).
#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
fn decode_inter_plane4(
    dst: &mut [u8],
    dst_off: usize,
    dst_stride: usize,
    ref_buf: &[u8],
    ref_stride: usize,
    bs: &mut BitReader,
    clamped_mvs: &[u32; 8],
    flags: u32,
    q: u32,
    cbp_state: &mut u32,
    scratch: &mut [u8; 128],
) {
    let cbp = decode_cbp4(bs, *cbp_state);
    *cbp_state = cbp;

    let ext_bit = (flags >> 18) & 1;
    let min_dc: i16 = if ext_bit != 0 { -2047 } else { -1023 };
    let max_dc: i16 = if ext_bit != 0 { 2047 } else { 1023 };
    let mut dc = [0i32; 4];
    decode_dc4(&mut dc, None, q, bs, 0x20a8, min_dc, max_dc);

    let mut coeffs = [0i16; 256];
    let mut info = [0u16; 4];
    let last_nz = decode_ac_quad(cbp, &mut coeffs, bs, q, &QMAT_INTER, &dc, &mut info, 0);

    let mut pred = [0u8; 256];
    let pred_offs = [0usize, 8, 128, 136];
    for q_idx in 0..4usize {
        let mx = clamped_mvs[q_idx * 2];
        let my = clamped_mvs[q_idx * 2 + 1];
        mc_chroma_8x8(
            ref_buf,
            ref_stride,
            (mx >> 2) as i32,
            (my >> 2) as i32,
            &mut pred,
            pred_offs[q_idx],
            16,
            mx & 3,
            my & 3,
        );
    }

    let mut res = [0i16; 256];
    idct_quad(&coeffs, last_nz, &mut res, 0, 16);

    if (flags & 0x44000) == 0 {
        smooth_inter_horiz(&mut res, 6, 16, info[0], info[1]);
        smooth_inter_horiz(&mut res, 128 + 6, 16, info[2], info[3]);
    }
    if (flags & 0x48000) == 0 {
        smooth_inter_vert(&mut res, 6 * 16, 16, info[0], info[2]);
        smooth_inter_vert(&mut res, 6 * 16 + 8, 16, info[1], info[3]);
    }

    if (flags & 4) != 0 {
        add_clamp_16x8(scratch, 0, 16, &res, 0, 16, &pred, 0, 16);
    } else {
        add_clamp_16x8(dst, dst_off, dst_stride, &res, 0, 16, &pred, 0, 16);
    }
    if (flags & 3) != 0 {
        add_clamp_16x8(scratch, 0, 16, &res, 128, 16, &pred, 128, 16);
    } else {
        add_clamp_16x8(
            dst,
            dst_off + dst_stride * 8,
            dst_stride,
            &res,
            128,
            16,
            &pred,
            128,
            16,
        );
    }
}

/// Decode motion vector deltas and apply spatial median prediction (`180014140`).
fn decode_mvs(
    bs: &mut BitReader,
    mv_cur: &mut [i32],
    mv_above: &[i32],
    mb_x_idx: usize,
    flags: u32,
) {
    let count = if bs.read_bit() != 0 { 1usize } else { 4usize };
    let base = mb_x_idx * 8;

    for axis in 0..2usize {
        let mut deltas = [0i32; 4];
        for slot in deltas.iter_mut().take(count) {
            bs.refill();
            let v = (bs.bit_buf as u32) & 0xffff;
            if (v & 0xf) != 0 {
                let e = MV_VLC[(v & 0x7f) as usize];
                let blen = (e as u8 & 7) as u32;
                bs.consume_fast(blen);
                *slot = (e >> 3) as i32;
            } else {
                let raw: u32 = if v == 0 {
                    bs.consume_fast(16);
                    bs.read_bits(16).wrapping_add(0xffff)
                } else {
                    let tz = v.trailing_zeros(); // 4..=15
                    let total_bits = tz * 2 + 1;
                    let w = (bs.bit_buf as u32) & ((1u32 << total_bits) - 1);
                    bs.consume_fast(total_bits);
                    (w >> (tz + 1)).wrapping_add((1u32 << tz) - 1)
                };
                let unzz = ((raw >> 1) as i32) ^ -((raw & 1) as i32);
                *slot = unzz;
            }
        }

        let top_edge = (flags & 0x88) != 0;
        let left_edge = (flags & 0x20) != 0;

        let (q0, q1, q2, q3) = if count == 1 {
            let pred = if !top_edge {
                let ab_q2 = mv_above[base + 4 + axis];
                if !left_edge {
                    let al_q3 = mv_above[base - 2 + axis];
                    let l_q1 = mv_cur[base - 6 + axis];
                    median3_i32(ab_q2, al_q3, l_q1)
                } else {
                    let ab_q3 = mv_above[base + 6 + axis];
                    let ab_q0 = mv_above[base + axis];
                    median3_i32(ab_q2, ab_q3, ab_q0)
                }
            } else if left_edge {
                0
            } else {
                let l_q3 = mv_cur[base - 2 + axis];
                let l_q1 = mv_cur[base - 6 + axis];
                let l_q0 = mv_cur[base - 8 + axis];
                median3_i32(l_q1, l_q0, l_q3)
            };
            let val = pred.wrapping_add(deltas[0]);
            (val, val, val, val)
        } else if !top_edge {
            if left_edge {
                let ab_q0 = mv_above[base + axis];
                let ab_q2 = mv_above[base + 4 + axis];
                let ab_q3 = mv_above[base + 6 + axis];
                let q0 = median3_i32(ab_q0, ab_q2, ab_q3).wrapping_add(deltas[0]);
                let q1 = median3_i32(q0, ab_q2, ab_q3).wrapping_add(deltas[1]);
                let q2 = median3_i32(q0, q1, ab_q2).wrapping_add(deltas[2]);
                let q3 = median3_i32(q0, q1, q2).wrapping_add(deltas[3]);
                (q0, q1, q2, q3)
            } else {
                let ab_q2 = mv_above[base + 4 + axis];
                let ab_q3 = mv_above[base + 6 + axis];
                let al_q3 = mv_above[base - 2 + axis];
                let l_q1 = mv_cur[base - 6 + axis];
                let l_q3 = mv_cur[base - 2 + axis];
                let q0 = median3_i32(l_q1, ab_q2, al_q3).wrapping_add(deltas[0]);
                let q1 = median3_i32(q0, ab_q2, ab_q3).wrapping_add(deltas[1]);
                let q2 = median3_i32(q0, l_q1, l_q3).wrapping_add(deltas[2]);
                let q3 = median3_i32(q0, q1, q2).wrapping_add(deltas[3]);
                (q0, q1, q2, q3)
            }
        } else if left_edge {
            let q0 = deltas[0];
            let q1 = deltas[1].wrapping_add(q0);
            let q2 = deltas[2].wrapping_add(q0);
            let q3 = median3_i32(q0, q1, q2).wrapping_add(deltas[3]);
            (q0, q1, q2, q3)
        } else {
            let l_q0 = mv_cur[base - 8 + axis];
            let l_q1 = mv_cur[base - 6 + axis];
            let l_q3 = mv_cur[base - 2 + axis];
            let q0 = median3_i32(l_q1, l_q0, l_q3).wrapping_add(deltas[0]);
            let q2 = median3_i32(q0, l_q1, l_q3).wrapping_add(deltas[2]);
            let q1 = median3_i32(q0, q2, l_q1).wrapping_add(deltas[1]);
            let q3 = median3_i32(q0, q1, q2).wrapping_add(deltas[3]);
            (q0, q1, q2, q3)
        };

        mv_cur[base + axis] = q0;
        mv_cur[base + 2 + axis] = q1;
        mv_cur[base + 4 + axis] = q2;
        mv_cur[base + 6 + axis] = q3;
    }
}

/// Fill MV predictors for an Intra MB in a P-frame (`180022ea0`).
fn intra_mv_median(mv_cur: &mut [i32], mv_above: &[i32], mb_x_idx: usize, flags: u32) {
    let base = mb_x_idx * 8;
    let top_edge = (flags & 0x88) != 0;
    let left_edge = (flags & 0x20) != 0;
    let is_int = (flags & 0x2000) != 0;

    for axis in 0..2usize {
        let (q0, q1, q2, q3) = if !top_edge {
            if !left_edge {
                let ab_q2 = mv_above[base + 4 + axis];
                let ab_q3 = mv_above[base + 6 + axis];
                let al_q3 = mv_above[base - 2 + axis];
                let l_q1 = mv_cur[base - 6 + axis];
                let l_q3 = mv_cur[base - 2 + axis];
                let q0 = median3_i32(l_q1, ab_q2, al_q3);
                let q1 = median3_i32(q0, ab_q3, ab_q2);
                let q2 = if is_int {
                    median3_i32(q0, l_q1, l_q3)
                } else {
                    median3_i32(q0, q1, ab_q2)
                };
                let q3 = median3_i32(q0, q1, q2);
                (q0, q1, q2, q3)
            } else {
                let ab_q0 = mv_above[base + axis];
                let ab_q2 = mv_above[base + 4 + axis];
                let ab_q3 = mv_above[base + 6 + axis];
                let q0 = median3_i32(ab_q0, ab_q2, ab_q3);
                let q1 = median3_i32(q0, ab_q2, ab_q3);
                let q2 = median3_i32(q0, q1, ab_q2);
                let q3 = median3_i32(q0, q1, q2);
                (q0, q1, q2, q3)
            }
        } else if left_edge {
            (0, 0, 0, 0)
        } else {
            let l_q0 = mv_cur[base - 8 + axis];
            let l_q1 = mv_cur[base - 6 + axis];
            let l_q3 = mv_cur[base - 2 + axis];
            let q0 = median3_i32(l_q0, l_q1, l_q3);
            let q2 = median3_i32(q0, l_q1, l_q3);
            let q1 = median3_i32(q0, q2, l_q1);
            let q3 = median3_i32(q0, q1, q2);
            (q0, q1, q2, q3)
        };
        mv_cur[base + axis] = q0;
        mv_cur[base + 2 + axis] = q1;
        mv_cur[base + 4 + axis] = q2;
        mv_cur[base + 6 + axis] = q3;
    }
}

/// Convert relative MVs to clamped absolute half-pel coordinates (`18001dc10`).
fn clamp_mvs(
    mv_cur: &[i32],
    mb_x_idx: usize,
    x_px: i32,
    y_px: i32,
    w2: i32,
    h2: i32,
    flags: u32,
) -> [u32; 8] {
    let base = mb_x_idx * 8;
    let mut out = [0u32; 8];
    let new_clamp = (flags & 0x20000) != 0;

    for q in 0..4usize {
        let qx = mv_cur[base + q * 2]
            .wrapping_add(((q & 1) as i32) * 32)
            .wrapping_add(x_px * 2);
        let qy = mv_cur[base + q * 2 + 1]
            .wrapping_add(((q >> 1) as i32) * 32)
            .wrapping_add(y_px * 2);

        let cx = clamp_axis(qx, w2, new_clamp);
        let cy = clamp_axis(qy, h2, new_clamp);
        out[q * 2] = cx;
        out[q * 2 + 1] = cy;
    }
    out
}

#[inline]
fn clamp_axis(val: i32, limit2: i32, new_clamp: bool) -> u32 {
    let odd = (val & 1) as usize;
    let span = limit2.wrapping_sub(MV_CLAMP[4 + odd]) as u32;
    if !new_clamp {
        let mn = MV_CLAMP[odd] as u32;
        let u = val as u32;
        if u.wrapping_sub(mn) > span {
            if u < mn {
                mn
            } else {
                span
            }
        } else {
            u
        }
    } else {
        let mn = MV_CLAMP[2 + odd];
        if (val.wrapping_sub(mn) as u32) > span {
            if val < mn {
                mn as u32
            } else {
                (mn as u32).wrapping_add(span)
            }
        } else {
            val as u32
        }
    }
}

/// Sliding-window post-filter & in-loop deblocking state (`18001e250`).
struct PostFilterState {
    // 4 rows of 8 u16s + 4 right-edge u16s per luma-sized plane (Y, A, H)
    y_win: [[u16; 8]; 4],
    y_right: [u16; 4],
    a_win: [[u16; 8]; 4],
    a_right: [u16; 4],
    h_win: [[u16; 8]; 4],
    h_right: [u16; 4],
    // 2 rows of 4 u16s + 2 right-edge u16s per chroma plane (Cr, Cb)
    cr_win: [[u16; 4]; 2],
    cr_right: [u16; 2],
    cb_win: [[u16; 4]; 2],
    cb_right: [u16; 2],
    // Delayed horizontal deblock masks (param_1[0x13] = [u32; 3])
    hdeb: [u32; 3],
}

impl PostFilterState {
    fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            y_win: [[0; 8]; 4],
            y_right: [0; 4],
            a_win: [[0; 8]; 4],
            a_right: [0; 4],
            h_win: [[0; 8]; 4],
            h_right: [0; 4],
            cr_win: [[0; 4]; 2],
            cr_right: [0; 2],
            cb_win: [[0; 4]; 2],
            cb_right: [0; 2],
            hdeb: [0; 3],
        }
    }
}

#[inline]
fn dc_pair_mask(left: u16, right: u16) -> u32 {
    let a = (left as u32).wrapping_add(0x2000) >> 1;
    let b = (right as u32).wrapping_add(0x2000) & 0x10000;
    (a | b) >> 15
}

#[inline]
fn deblock_idx(a: u16, b: u16) -> usize {
    ((((a >> 2) & 0x1800) | (b & 0x6000)) >> 11) as usize
}

#[inline]
fn vert_weight_idx(a: u16, b: u16) -> usize {
    // DEBLOCK_LUT_A returns byte offset into 16-byte weights table divided by 2 (0, 8, 16, ... 112),
    // which is `weight_index * 8`.
    (DEBLOCK_LUT_A[deblock_idx(a, b)] >> 3) as usize
}

#[inline]
fn horiz_pair_byte(a0: u16, b0: u16, a1: u16, b1: u16) -> u8 {
    DEBLOCK_LUT_B[deblock_idx(a0, b0)] | DEBLOCK_LUT_C[deblock_idx(a1, b1)]
}

#[inline]
fn mv_diff_gt1(a: i32, b: i32) -> bool {
    (a.wrapping_sub(b).wrapping_add(1) as u32) > 2
}

#[inline]
fn apply_hdeb_byte(dst: &mut [u8], off: usize, stride: usize, mask_byte: u8) {
    if mask_byte != 0 {
        let lw = (mask_byte & 0xf) as usize;
        let rw = (mask_byte >> 4) as usize;
        deblock_horiz_16(dst, off, stride, lw, rw);
    }
}

/// Apply delayed horizontal deblocks from the previous MB's right half (`18001df20`).
fn flush_delayed_hdeb(
    y_buf: &mut [u8],
    y_off_m2: usize,
    y_stride: usize,
    cr_buf: &mut [u8],
    cr_off_m2: usize,
    cb_buf: &mut [u8],
    cb_off_m2: usize,
    c_stride: usize,
    hdeb: [u32; 3],
    x_right_half: bool,
) {
    // When called mid-row (`x_right_half == false`), `y_off_m2` is at `(x - 32, y - 2)`,
    // and delayed right-half edges sit at `x - 32 - 16` = `(prev_prev_mb.x + 16)`.
    // When called at end-of-row flush (`x_right_half == true`), `y_off_m2` is at `(x, y - 2)`
    // and edges sit at `(x + 16)` (Wait: `pcVar17` was advanced by `+32`, so `pcVar17 + 16`!).
    let y_dx: isize = if x_right_half { 16 } else { -16 };
    let c_dx: isize = if x_right_half { 0 } else { -16 };
